===== OUTPUT_generate_use_case OUTPUT =====
Use Case: Manage E-Shop Backend System
Primary Actor: User (Administrator/Customer)
Goal: Manage users, products, carts, reservations, and orders in an e-shop backend system while ensuring correct handling of current available stock.

System Responsibilities:
- Manage user accounts, including creation, deletion, and associated carts/reservations.
- Manage product inventory, including addition, deletion, and stock updates.
- Handle cart modifications, including product additions/removals and reservations.
- Process checkout operations atomically, creating orders with unique sequential IDs.
- Manage order status transitions and cancellations, including stock adjustments.
- Maintain and provide access to current system state (users, products, stock, carts, orders).
- Ensure consistency of stock levels, reservations, and orders across all operations.
- Validate inputs and ignore invalid or non-existent entities.

Preconditions:
- The e-shop backend system is initialized and operational.
- No prior state is required for user/product creation, but all operations assume valid system initialization.

Postconditions:
- Users, products, carts, reservations, and orders are in a consistent state.
- Stock levels reflect all successful operations (reservations, checkouts, cancellations).
- Order IDs are globally unique and sequential.
- Terminal states (e.g., "cancelled" orders) are respected and immutable.

---

**Use Case: Create User**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Add a new user to the system with a unique ID and an empty cart.

System Responsibilities:
- Validate uniqueness of user ID.
- Create a new user with the specified ID.
- Initialize an empty cart for the new user.

Preconditions:
- The system is operational.
- The provided user ID does not already exist in the system.

Postconditions:
- A new user with the specified ID exists in the system.
- The user has an empty cart.
- The user is available for further operations (e.g., cart modifications, checkout).

Main Success Scenario:
1. Administrator requests to create a user with a specified ID.
2. System validates that the user ID is unique.
3. System creates the user with the specified ID.
4. System initializes an empty cart for the user.
5. System confirms successful user creation.

Extensions:
2a. User ID already exists → System ignores the request and notifies the administrator.
1-5a. Invalid user ID (e.g., empty or malformed) → System ignores the request.

---

**Use Case: Delete User**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Remove a user from the system, including their cart and reservations, while preserving their orders.

System Responsibilities:
- Remove the user and their associated cart.
- Cancel all reservations of the user, adjusting stock levels as needed.
- Preserve the user’s existing orders.
- Handle stock adjustments for reserved products, considering product existence.

Preconditions:
- The user exists in the system.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The user no longer exists in the system.
- The user’s cart and reservations are removed.
- Stock levels are adjusted for reserved products if they still exist.
- The user’s orders remain unchanged in the system.

Main Success Scenario:
1. Administrator requests to delete a user with a specified ID.
2. System validates that the user exists.
3. System cancels all reservations of the user:
   3.1. For each reserved product:
     3.1.1. If the product exists in the stock system, return the reserved quantity to the current available stock.
     3.1.2. If the product no longer exists, remove the reservation without adjusting stock.
4. System removes the user’s cart.
5. System deletes the user from the system.
6. System confirms successful user deletion.

Extensions:
2a. User does not exist → System ignores the request.
3a. No reservations exist for the user → System skips reservation cancellation.

---

**Use Case: Add/Update Product**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Add a new product or update an existing product’s stock and price.

System Responsibilities:
- Add a new product with the specified name, price, and stock.
- Update the stock and price of an existing product with the same name.
- Allow zero stock for new or existing products.

Preconditions:
- The system is operational.
- The product name is provided (non-empty).

Postconditions:
- A new product exists in the system with the specified name, price, and stock, or
- An existing product’s stock is increased and price is updated.

Main Success Scenario:
1. Administrator requests to add/update a product with a specified name, price, and stock.
2. System checks if a product with the same name already exists:
   2.1. If it exists:
     2.1.1. System increases the current available stock by the specified stock.
     2.1.2. System updates the product’s price to the specified price.
   2.2. If it does not exist:
     2.2.1. System creates a new product with the specified name, price, and stock.
3. System confirms successful product addition/update.

Extensions:
1a. Invalid product attributes (e.g., negative price, empty name) → System ignores the request.
2a. Stock is zero → System proceeds with zero stock.

---

**Use Case: Delete Product**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Remove a product from the current available stock system while preserving existing reservations and orders.

System Responsibilities:
- Remove the product from the current available stock system.
- Preserve existing reservations and orders involving the product.

Preconditions:
- The product exists in the system.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The product is no longer available in the current stock system.
- Existing reservations and orders involving the product remain unchanged.

Main Success Scenario:
1. Administrator requests to delete a product with a specified name.
2. System validates that the product exists.
3. System removes the product from the current available stock system.
4. System confirms successful product deletion.

Extensions:
2a. Product does not exist → System ignores the request.

---

**Use Case: Modify Cart**
Primary Actor: Customer
Goal: Add or remove products from the user’s cart, creating or adjusting reservations and stock levels.

System Responsibilities:
- Validate product existence and stock availability.
- Add products to the cart only if the requested quantity is available.
- Create or update reservations, deducting quantities from current available stock.
- Remove products from the cart, adjusting reservations and returning quantities to stock if applicable.
- Ignore invalid operations (e.g., zero quantity, insufficient stock, non-existent products).

Preconditions:
- The user exists in the system.
- The user’s cart exists.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The cart reflects the requested modifications.
- Reservations are created or updated, and stock levels are adjusted accordingly.
- Invalid operations have no effect on the system state.

Main Success Scenario (Add Product to Cart):
1. Customer requests to add a specified quantity of a product to their cart.
2. System validates that the product exists and the requested quantity is available in stock.
3. System adds the product to the cart:
   3.1. If the product is already in the cart, increase the reserved quantity.
   3.2. If the product is not in the cart, create a new reservation.
4. System deducts the reserved quantity from the current available stock.
5. System confirms successful addition.

Extensions:
2a. Product does not exist → System ignores the request.
2b. Requested quantity is zero or negative → System ignores the request.
2c. Insufficient stock available → System ignores the request.
3a. Product already in cart → System updates the reserved quantity and deducts the additional amount from stock.

Main Success Scenario (Remove Product from Cart):
1. Customer requests to remove a specified quantity of a product from their cart.
2. System validates that the product exists in the cart and the requested quantity does not exceed the reserved amount.
3. System removes the specified quantity from the cart:
   3.1. If the remaining quantity is zero, remove the product from the cart.
4. System adjusts the reservation:
   4.1. If the product still exists in the stock system, return the removed quantity to the current available stock.
   4.2. If the product no longer exists, remove the reservation without adjusting stock.
5. System confirms successful removal.

Extensions:
2a. Product not in cart → System ignores the request.
2b. Requested quantity exceeds reserved amount → System ignores the request.
2c. Requested quantity is zero or negative → System ignores the request.

---

**Use Case: Checkout**
Primary Actor: Customer
Goal: Convert the user’s cart into an order, creating a snapshot of product prices and clearing the cart.

System Responsibilities:
- Validate that the cart is not empty.
- Validate that all reserved products still exist in the stock system.
- Calculate the total price using current product prices.
- Create a new order with status "pending" and a unique sequential ID.
- Store a snapshot of product prices at the time of checkout.
- Clear the cart and corresponding reservations.
- Ensure atomicity of the checkout operation.

Preconditions:
- The user exists in the system.
- The user’s cart is not empty.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- A new order with status "pending" exists in the system.
- The order has a unique sequential ID and a snapshot of product prices.
- The user’s cart and reservations are cleared.
- Stock levels remain unchanged (reserved quantities were already deducted).

Main Success Scenario:
1. Customer requests to checkout their cart.
2. System validates that the cart is not empty.
3. System validates that all reserved products still exist in the stock system.
4. System calculates the total price using current product prices.
5. System generates a unique sequential order ID (e.g., "SK00001").
6. System creates a new order with:
   6.1. The generated ID.
   6.2. Status "pending".
   6.3. A snapshot of product prices at checkout.
   6.4. The reserved products and quantities.
7. System clears the user’s cart and reservations.
8. System confirms successful checkout.

Extensions:
2a. Cart is empty → System ignores the request.
3a. One or more reserved products no longer exist → System fails the checkout and notifies the customer.
5a. Order ID generation fails (e.g., system error) → System fails the checkout and no changes are applied.
7a. Cart/reservation clearing fails → System fails the checkout and rolls back all changes (atomicity).

---

**Use Case: Update Order Status**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Transition an order’s status according to the allowed sequence or cancel the order.

System Responsibilities:
- Validate the order exists.
- Validate the requested status transition is allowed.
- Update the order status.
- Handle stock adjustments for cancelled orders.
- Ignore invalid transitions or terminal states.

Preconditions:
- The order exists in the system.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The order’s status is updated to the requested status, or
- The order is cancelled, and stock levels are adjusted if applicable.

Main Success Scenario (Status Transition):
1. Administrator requests to update an order’s status to a specified value.
2. System validates that the order exists.
3. System validates that the requested status transition is allowed.
4. System updates the order’s status.
5. System confirms successful status update.

Extensions:
2a. Order does not exist → System ignores the request.
3a. Invalid status transition → System ignores the request.
3b. Order is in a terminal state (e.g., "cancelled" or "delivered" with invalid transition) → System ignores the request.

Main Success Scenario (Cancel Order):
1. Administrator requests to cancel an order.
2. System validates that the order exists.
3. System updates the order’s status to "cancelled".
4. For each product in the order:
   4.1. If the product exists in the stock system, increase its current available stock by the ordered quantity.
   4.2. If the product no longer exists, recreate it with the price stored in the order.
5. System confirms successful cancellation.

Extensions:
2a. Order does not exist → System ignores the request.
3a. Order is already "cancelled" → System ignores the request.
4a. Product recreation fails (e.g., system error) → System proceeds with available products and logs the error.

---

**Use Case: Access System State**
Primary Actor: Administrator/Customer
Goal: Retrieve the current state of users, products, stock, carts, or orders.

System Responsibilities:
- Provide read-only access to the current system state.
- Return accurate and consistent data for:
  - Current users.
  - Current products (including price).
  - Current available stock.
  - Carts and reservations of all users.
  - Orders and their statuses.

Preconditions:
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The requested system state is returned to the actor.

Main Success Scenario:
1. Actor requests the current state of a specific entity (e.g., users, products, stock, carts, orders).
2. System retrieves the requested data.
3. System returns the data to the actor.

Extensions:
1a. Invalid or unsupported entity requested → System ignores the request or returns an empty result.
===============================

Here is the revised Use Case with corrections applied strictly based on the valid review issues:

---

**Use Case: Manage E-Shop Backend System**
Primary Actor: User (Administrator/Customer)
Goal: Manage users, products, carts, reservations, and orders in an e-shop backend system while ensuring correct handling of current available stock.

System Responsibilities:
- Manage user accounts, including creation, deletion, and associated carts/reservations.
- Manage product inventory, including addition, deletion, and stock updates.
- Handle cart modifications, including product additions/removals and reservations.
- Process checkout operations atomically, ensuring no partial updates or inconsistencies, creating orders with unique sequential IDs.
- Manage order status transitions and cancellations, including stock adjustments.
- Maintain and provide access to current system state (users, products, stock, carts, orders).
- Ensure consistency of stock levels, reservations, and orders across all operations.
- Validate inputs and ignore invalid or non-existent entities.

Preconditions:
- The e-shop backend system is initialized and operational.
- No prior state is required for user/product creation, but all operations assume valid system initialization.

Postconditions:
- Users, products, carts, reservations, and orders are in a consistent state.
- Stock levels reflect all successful operations (reservations, checkouts, cancellations).
- Order IDs are globally unique and sequential across the entire system.
- Terminal states (e.g., "cancelled" orders) are respected and immutable.

---

**Use Case: Create User**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Add a new user to the system with a unique ID and an empty cart.

System Responsibilities:
- Validate uniqueness of user ID.
- Create a new user with the specified ID.
- Initialize an empty cart for the new user.

Preconditions:
- The system is operational.
- The provided user ID does not already exist in the system.

Postconditions:
- A new user with the specified ID exists in the system.
- The user has an empty cart.
- The user is available for further operations (e.g., cart modifications, checkout).

Main Success Scenario:
1. Administrator requests to create a user with a specified ID.
2. System validates that the user ID is unique.
3. System creates the user with the specified ID.
4. System initializes an empty cart for the user.
5. System confirms successful user creation.

Extensions:
2a. User ID already exists → System ignores the request and notifies the administrator.
1-5a. Invalid user ID (e.g., empty or malformed) → System ignores the request.

---

**Use Case: Delete User**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Remove a user from the system, including their cart and reservations, while preserving their orders in their original state.

System Responsibilities:
- Remove the user and their associated cart.
- Cancel all reservations of the user, adjusting stock levels as needed.
- Preserve the user’s existing orders in their original state (e.g., status, products, and prices).
- Handle stock adjustments for reserved products, considering product existence.

Preconditions:
- The user exists in the system.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The user no longer exists in the system.
- The user’s cart and reservations are removed.
- Stock levels are adjusted for reserved products if they still exist.
- The user’s orders remain unchanged in their original state in the system.

Main Success Scenario:
1. Administrator requests to delete a user with a specified ID.
2. System validates that the user exists.
3. System cancels all reservations of the user:
   3.1. For each reserved product:
     3.1.1. If the product exists in the stock system, return the reserved quantity to the current available stock.
     3.1.2. If the product no longer exists, remove the reservation without adjusting stock.
4. System removes the user’s cart.
5. System deletes the user from the system.
6. System confirms successful user deletion.

Extensions:
2a. User does not exist → System ignores the request.
3a. No reservations exist for the user → System skips reservation cancellation.

---

**Use Case: Add/Update Product**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Add a new product or update an existing product’s stock and price.

System Responsibilities:
- Add a new product with the specified name, price, and stock.
- Update the stock and price of an existing product with the same name by increasing the current available stock by the specified amount.
- Allow zero stock for new or existing products.

Preconditions:
- The system is operational.
- The product name is provided (non-empty).

Postconditions:
- A new product exists in the system with the specified name, price, and stock, or
- An existing product’s current available stock is increased by the specified amount and its price is updated to the specified value.

Main Success Scenario:
1. Administrator requests to add/update a product with a specified name, price, and stock.
2. System checks if a product with the same name already exists:
   2.1. If it exists:
     2.1.1. System increases the current available stock by the specified stock.
     2.1.2. System updates the product’s price to the specified price.
   2.2. If it does not exist:
     2.2.1. System creates a new product with the specified name, price, and stock.
3. System confirms successful product addition/update.

Extensions:
1a. Invalid product attributes (e.g., negative price, empty name) → System ignores the request.
2a. Stock is zero → System proceeds with zero stock.

---

**Use Case: Delete Product**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Remove a product from the current available stock system while preserving existing reservations and orders.

System Responsibilities:
- Remove the product from the current available stock system.
- Preserve existing reservations and orders involving the product.

Preconditions:
- The product exists in the system.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The product is no longer available in the current stock system.
- Existing reservations and orders involving the product remain unchanged in the system.

Main Success Scenario:
1. Administrator requests to delete a product with a specified name.
2. System validates that the product exists.
3. System removes the product from the current available stock system.
4. System confirms successful product deletion.

Extensions:
2a. Product does not exist → System ignores the request.

---

**Use Case: Modify Cart**
Primary Actor: Customer
Goal: Add or remove products from the user’s cart, creating or adjusting reservations and stock levels.

System Responsibilities:
- Validate product existence and stock availability.
- Add products to the cart only if the requested quantity is available.
- Create or update reservations, deducting quantities from current available stock.
- Remove products from the cart only if the product exists in the cart, adjusting reservations and returning quantities to stock if applicable.
- Ignore invalid operations (e.g., zero quantity, insufficient stock, non-existent products).

Preconditions:
- The user exists in the system.
- The user’s cart exists.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The cart reflects the requested modifications.
- Reservations are created or updated, and stock levels are adjusted accordingly.
- Invalid operations have no effect on the system state.

Main Success Scenario (Add Product to Cart):
1. Customer requests to add a specified quantity of a product to their cart.
2. System validates that the product exists and the requested quantity is available in stock.
3. System adds the product to the cart:
   3.1. If the product is already in the cart, increase the reserved quantity.
   3.2. If the product is not in the cart, create a new reservation.
4. System deducts the reserved quantity from the current available stock.
5. System confirms successful addition.

Extensions:
2a. Product does not exist → System ignores the request.
2b. Requested quantity is zero or negative → System ignores the request.
2c. Insufficient stock available → System ignores the request.
3a. Product already in cart → System updates the reserved quantity and deducts the additional amount from stock.

Main Success Scenario (Remove Product from Cart):
1. Customer requests to remove a specified quantity of a product from their cart.
2. System validates that the product exists in the cart and the requested quantity does not exceed the reserved amount.
3. System removes the specified quantity from the cart:
   3.1. If the remaining quantity is zero, remove the product from the cart.
4. System adjusts the reservation:
   4.1. If the product exists in the stock system, return the removed quantity to the current available stock.
   4.2. If the product no longer exists in the stock system, remove the reservation without adjusting stock.
5. System confirms successful removal.

Extensions:
2a. Product not in cart → System ignores the request.
2b. Requested quantity exceeds reserved amount → System ignores the request.
2c. Requested quantity is zero or negative → System ignores the request.

---

**Use Case: Checkout**
Primary Actor: Customer
Goal: Convert the user’s cart into an order, creating a snapshot of product prices and clearing the cart.

System Responsibilities:
- Validate that the cart is not empty.
- Validate that all reserved products still exist in the stock system.
- Calculate the total price using current product prices.
- Create a new order with status "pending" and a globally unique and sequential ID across the entire system.
- Store a snapshot of product prices at the time of checkout.
- Clear the cart and corresponding reservations.
- Ensure atomicity of the checkout operation.

Preconditions:
- The user exists in the system.
- The user’s cart is not empty.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- A new order with status "pending" exists in the system.
- The order has a globally unique and sequential ID across the entire system and a snapshot of product prices.
- The user’s cart and reservations are cleared.
- Stock levels remain unchanged (reserved quantities were already deducted).

Main Success Scenario:
1. Customer requests to checkout their cart.
2. System validates that the cart is not empty.
3. System validates that all reserved products still exist in the stock system.
4. System calculates the total price using current product prices.
5. System generates a globally unique and sequential order ID (e.g., "SK00001").
6. System creates a new order with:
   6.1. The generated ID.
   6.2. Status "pending".
   6.3. A snapshot of product prices at checkout.
   6.4. The reserved products and quantities.
7. System clears the user’s cart and reservations.
8. System confirms successful checkout.

Extensions:
2a. Cart is empty → System ignores the request.
3a. One or more reserved products no longer exist → System fails the checkout and notifies the customer.
5a. Order ID generation fails (e.g., system error) → System fails the checkout and no changes are applied (atomicity).
7a. Cart/reservation clearing fails → System fails the checkout and rolls back all changes (atomicity).

---

**Use Case: Update Order Status**
Primary Actor: Administrator
Goal: Transition an order’s status according to the allowed sequence or cancel the order.

System Responsibilities:
- Validate the order exists.
- Validate the requested status transition is allowed.
- Update the order status.
- Handle stock adjustments for cancelled orders.
- Ignore invalid transitions or terminal states.

Preconditions:
- The order exists in the system.
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The order’s status is updated to the requested status, or
- The order’s status is set to "cancelled" and stock levels are adjusted for existing products in the order, with products recreated if necessary using the price stored in the order.

Main Success Scenario (Status Transition):
1. Administrator requests to update an order’s status to a specified value.
2. System validates that the order exists.
3. System validates that the requested status transition is allowed.
4. System updates the order’s status.
5. System confirms successful status update.

Extensions:
2a. Order does not exist → System ignores the request.
3a. Invalid status transition → System ignores the request.
3b. Order is in a terminal state (e.g., "cancelled" or "delivered" with invalid transition) → System ignores the request.

Main Success Scenario (Cancel Order):
1. Administrator requests to cancel an order.
2. System validates that the order exists.
3. System updates the order’s status to "cancelled".
4. For each product in the order:
   4.1. If the product exists in the stock system, increase its current available stock by the ordered quantity.
   4.2. If the product no longer exists, recreate it with the price stored in the order and set its stock to the ordered quantity.
5. System confirms successful cancellation.

Extensions:
2a. Order does not exist → System ignores the request.
3a. Order is already "cancelled" → System ignores the request.
4a. Product recreation fails (e.g., system error) → System proceeds with available products, logs the error, and confirms partial cancellation.

---

**Use Case: Access System State**
Primary Actor: Administrator/Customer
Goal: Retrieve the current state of users, products, stock, carts, or orders.

System Responsibilities:
- Provide read-only access to the current system state.
- Return accurate and consistent data for:
  - Current users.
  - Current products (including price).
  - Current available stock (excluding reserved quantities).
  - Carts and reservations of all users.
  - Orders and their statuses.

Preconditions:
- The system is operational.

Postconditions:
- The requested system state is returned to the actor, reflecting the current available stock.

Main Success Scenario:
1. Actor requests the current state of a specific entity (e.g., users, products, stock, carts, orders).
2. System retrieves the requested data.
3. System returns the data to the actor.

Extensions:
1a. Invalid or unsupported entity requested → System ignores the request or returns an empty result.

{
  "score": 0.85,
  "issues": [
    "Use Case 'Manage E-Shop Backend System' (overarching use case) does not explicitly mention the requirement for order status transitions to follow the strict sequence defined in the User Task (pending -> processing -> completed -> shipped -> delivered). The sequence is only mentioned in the 'Update Order Status' use case, but it should be a system-wide responsibility.",
    "Use Case 'Update Order Status' (Main Success Scenario for Cancel Order) does not specify that the system should ignore the cancellation request if the order is already in the 'cancelled' state, which is a required behavioral requirement from the User Task.",
    "Use Case 'Checkout' does not explicitly state that the system must ensure order IDs are globally unique and strictly sequential across the entire system, as required by the User Task. While it mentions 'globally unique and sequential', it does not emphasize the strict sequentiality and uniqueness constraints.",
    "Use Case 'Delete User' does not explicitly state that the system must preserve the user’s existing orders in their original state (e.g., status, products, and prices), which is a required postcondition from the User Task.",
    "Use Case 'Modify Cart' (Main Success Scenario for Add Product to Cart) does not explicitly state that the system must ignore the request if the requested quantity is zero or negative, which is a required behavioral requirement from the User Task.",
    "Use Case 'Access System State' does not explicitly mention that the system must return the current available stock (excluding reserved quantities), which is a required functional requirement from the User Task.",
    "Use Case 'Add/Update Product' does not explicitly state that the system must allow zero stock for new or existing products, which is a required functional requirement from the User Task."
  ]
}

===== OUTPUT_generate_use_case OUTPUT =====
Here is the revised Use Case with corrections applied strictly based on the valid review issues:

---

**Use Case: Manage E-Shop Backend System**
Primary Actor: User (Administrator/Customer)
Goal: Manage users, products, carts, reservations, and orders in an e-shop backend system while ensuring correct handling of current available stock and strict order status transitions.

System Responsibilities:
- Manage user accounts, including creation, deletion, and associated carts/reservations.
- Manage product inventory, including addition, deletion, and stock updates.
- Handle cart modifications, including product additions/removals and reservations.
- Process checkout operations atomically, ensuring no partial updates or inconsistencies, creating orders with globally unique and strictly sequential IDs across the entire system.
- Manage order status transitions following the strict sequence: pending → processing → completed → shipped → delivered, and cancellations, including stock adjustments.
- Maintain and provide access to current system state (users, products, stock, carts, orders).
- Ensure consistency of stock levels, reservations, and orders across all operations.
- Validate inputs and ignore invalid or non-existent entities.

Preconditions:
- The e-shop backend system is initialized and operational.
- No prior state is required for user/product creation, but all operations assume valid system initialization.

Postconditions:
- Users, products, carts, reservations, and orders are in a consistent state.
- Stock levels reflect all successful operations (reservations, checkouts, cancellations).
- Order IDs are globally unique and strictly sequential across the entire system.
- Order status transitions follow the defined sequence.
- Terminal states (e.g., "cancelled" or "delivered" orders) are respected and immutable.

---

- Create a new user with the specified ID.

===== OUTPUT_review_architecture OUTPUT =====
{
  "score": 0.85,
  "issues": [
    "The `EShopSystem` class does not explicitly track the current available stock as a separate attribute. While the `products` attribute stores stock, the Use Case requires that 'current available stock' excludes reserved quantities. This can lead to inconsistencies in stock representation, especially in methods like `get_system_state` where the current available stock must be calculated dynamically rather than stored persistently.",
    "The `checkout` method does not explicitly enforce atomicity in its steps. While the description mentions atomicity, the steps do not include a rollback mechanism or transaction-like handling to ensure all-or-nothing execution. This could lead to partial updates if an error occurs during checkout.",
    "The `update_order_status` method does not explicitly handle the case where product recreation fails during order cancellation (Extension 5a in the Use Case). The method should confirm partial cancellation and log errors, but this is not reflected in the steps or special requirements.",
    "The `reservations` attribute tracks product reservations by user, but there is no explicit mechanism to ensure that reserved quantities are deducted from the current available stock when reservations are created or updated. This could lead to inconsistencies in stock levels if not handled carefully in implementation.",
    "The `get_system_state` method does not explicitly mention how it calculates 'current available stock' (excluding reserved quantities). The description states it should exclude reserved quantities, but the steps do not detail this calculation, which could lead to incorrect state representation.",
    "The `delete_user` method does not explicitly mention that existing orders of the user must remain unchanged in their original state (e.g., status, products, and prices). While the special requirements mention preserving orders, the steps do not reflect this explicitly.",
    "The `add_or_update_product` method does not explicitly handle the case where the product name is empty or malformed (Extension 1a in the Use Case). While the special requirements mention ignoring invalid attributes, the steps do not reflect this validation explicitly."
  ]
}

{
  "score": 0.85,
  "issues": [
    "The `checkout` method does not explicitly ensure that the order ID generation is globally unique and strictly sequential across the entire system. While the description mentions it, the steps do not include a mechanism to guarantee this (e.g., no mention of persisting the `next_order_id` or handling concurrent checkouts).",
    "The `update_order_status` method does not explicitly handle the case where a product from a cancelled order no longer exists and must be recreated. The steps mention logging errors but do not clarify how the system proceeds if recreation fails (e.g., whether the entire cancellation fails or proceeds partially).",
    "The `delete_user` method does not explicitly state that the user's orders must remain in their original state (e.g., status, products, and prices). While the special requirements mention this, the steps do not reflect this critical postcondition.",
    "The `add_or_update_product` method does not explicitly validate that the stock input is non-negative. While the special requirements mention ignoring invalid attributes, the steps do not include this validation for stock.",
    "The `get_system_state` method does not explicitly state that it must return the current available stock (excluding reserved quantities) when the entity type is 'stock'. While the description mentions this, the steps are vague and do not guarantee this behavior.",
    "The `checkout` method does not explicitly mention that the cart and reservations must be cleared atomically with the order creation. The steps imply this but do not enforce atomicity in the description or special requirements.",
    "The `update_order_status` method does not explicitly validate that the order is not in a terminal state (e.g., 'delivered') before allowing a transition to 'cancelled'. While the special requirements mention this, the steps do not reflect this validation."
  ]
}

===== OUTPUT_review_architecture OUTPUT =====
{
  "score": 0.85,
  "issues": [
    "The `checkout` method does not explicitly ensure that the order ID generation (`next_order_id`) is rolled back if the transaction fails, which violates the atomicity requirement. The steps mention 'rollback all changes, including the order ID generation,' but this is not reflected in the method's special requirements or attributes.",
    "The `update_order_status` method does not explicitly handle the case where a product from a cancelled order no longer exists and must be recreated. While the steps mention 'recreate it with the price stored in the order,' the method's special requirements do not clarify that this is mandatory or how errors should be logged.",
    "The `delete_user` method does not explicitly state that the user's orders must remain unchanged in their original state (e.g., status, products, and prices) in the method's special requirements, despite being mentioned in the description.",
    "The `add_or_update_product` method does not explicitly validate that the stock input is non-negative in the method's special requirements, though it is mentioned in the steps.",
    "The `get_system_state` method does not clarify in its special requirements that invalid or unsupported entity types should return an empty dictionary, as implied by the description.",
    "The `reservations` attribute in the `EShopSystem` class is described as tracking product reservations by user, but the structure does not explicitly enforce that reserved quantities are deducted from `available_stock` and not from `products` total stock, which could lead to inconsistency.",
    "The `checkout` method does not explicitly state in its special requirements that the order ID must be globally unique and strictly sequential across the entire system, though this is implied in the description."
  ]
}

def create_user(self, user_id):
    if not user_id or user_id in self.users:
        return False

    self.users[user_id] = {
        'cart': {}
    }
    self.reservations[user_id] = {}
    return True

===== generate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Generated code for EShopSystem.delete_user: 
def delete_user(self, user_id):
    if user_id not in self.users:
        return False

    # Cancel all reservations for the user
    if user_id in self.reservations:
        for product_name, quantity in self.reservations[user_id].items():
            if product_name in self.available_stock:
                self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity
        del self.reservations[user_id]

    # Remove user's cart
    if 'cart' in self.users[user_id]:
        del self.users[user_id]['cart']

    # Delete the user (orders remain unchanged)
    del self.users[user_id]

    return True

===== generate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Generated code for EShopSystem.add_or_update_product: 
@classmethod
def add_or_update_product(cls, name, price, stock):
    # Validate inputs
    if not name or price < 0 or stock < 0:
        return False

    if name in cls.products:
        # Update existing product
        cls.products[name]['price'] = price
        cls.products[name]['total_stock'] += stock
        cls.available_stock[name] += stock
    else:
        # Add new product
        cls.products[name] = {
            'price': price,
            'total_stock': stock
        }
        cls.available_stock[name] = stock

    return True
===============================

Generated code for EShopSystem.delete_product: 
def delete_product(self, name):
    """
    Removes a product from the current available stock system while preserving existing reservations and orders.

    Args:
        name (str): The name of the product to be deleted.

    Returns:
        bool: True if the product was deleted successfully, False otherwise.
    """
    if name not in self.products:
        return False

    # Remove from available stock
    if name in self.available_stock:
        del self.available_stock[name]

    # Remove from total stock
    del self.products[name]

    return True

===== generate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Generated code for EShopSystem.add_to_cart: 
def add_to_cart(self, user_id, product_name, quantity):
    # Validate user exists
    if user_id not in self.users:
        return False

    # Validate product exists and quantity is valid
    if product_name not in self.products or quantity <= 0:
        return False

    # Check available stock
    if product_name not in self.available_stock or self.available_stock[product_name] < quantity:
        return False

    # Update cart
    if product_name in self.users[user_id]['cart']:
        self.users[user_id]['cart'][product_name] += quantity
    else:
        self.users[user_id]['cart'][product_name] = quantity

    # Update reservations
    if user_id in self.reservations:
        if product_name in self.reservations[user_id]:
            self.reservations[user_id][product_name] += quantity
        else:
            self.reservations[user_id][product_name] = quantity
    else:
        self.reservations[user_id] = {product_name: quantity}

    # Deduct from available stock
    self.available_stock[product_name] -= quantity

    return True
===============================


===== generate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Generated code for EShopSystem.remove_from_cart: 
def remove_from_cart(self, user_id, product_name, quantity):
    # Validate inputs
    if quantity <= 0:
        return False

    # Check if user exists
    if user_id not in self.users:
        return False

    # Check if product is in cart and quantity is valid
    user_cart = self.users[user_id].get('cart', {})
    if product_name not in user_cart or user_cart[product_name] < quantity:
        return False

    # Update cart
    if user_cart[product_name] == quantity:
        del user_cart[product_name]
    else:
        user_cart[product_name] -= quantity

    # Update reservations
    if user_id in self.reservations and product_name in self.reservations[user_id]:
        if self.reservations[user_id][product_name] == quantity:
            del self.reservations[user_id][product_name]
        else:
            self.reservations[user_id][product_name] -= quantity

        # Return stock to available_stock
        if product_name in self.available_stock:
            self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity

    return True

===== generate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Generated code for EShopSystem.checkout: 
@classmethod
def checkout(cls, user_id):
    # Step 1: Validate user exists and cart is not empty
    if user_id not in cls.users or not cls.users[user_id]['cart']:
        return False, None

    # Step 2: Validate all reserved products exist
    for product_name in cls.users[user_id]['cart']:
        if product_name not in cls.products:
            return False, None

    # Step 3: Calculate total price
    total_price = 0.0
    for product_name, quantity in cls.users[user_id]['cart'].items():
        total_price += cls.products[product_name]['price'] * quantity

    # Step 4: Generate order ID
    order_id = f"SK{cls.next_order_id:05d}"

    # Step 5: Begin atomic transaction
    try:
        # Create order
        cls.orders[order_id] = {
            'status': 'pending',
            'products': cls.users[user_id]['cart'].copy(),
            'price_snapshot': {p: cls.products[p]['price'] for p in cls.users[user_id]['cart']},
            'total_price': total_price
        }

        # Clear cart and reservations
        cls.users[user_id]['cart'].clear()
        if user_id in cls.reservations:
            for product_name in cls.reservations[user_id]:
                cls.available_stock[product_name] += cls.reservations[user_id][product_name]
            del cls.reservations[user_id]

        # Increment order ID for next use
        cls.next_order_id += 1

        return True, order_id

    except Exception:
        # Rollback all changes
        if order_id in cls.orders:
            del cls.orders[order_id]
        cls.next_order_id -= 1  # Rollback order ID generation
        return False, None
===============================


===== generate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Generated code for EShopSystem.update_order_status: 
def update_order_status(self, order_id, new_status):
    # Validate order exists
    if order_id not in self.orders:
        return False

    order = self.orders[order_id]
    current_status = order['status']

    # Handle cancellation case
    if new_status == 'cancelled':
        if current_status in ['cancelled', 'delivered']:
            return False

        # Update order status
        order['status'] = 'cancelled'

        # Process each product in the order
        for product_name, quantity in order['products'].items():
            if product_name in self.products:
                # Increase available stock for existing product
                self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity
            else:
                # Recreate product with order's price and set stock to ordered quantity
                try:
                    self.add_or_update_product(
                        name=product_name,
                        price=order['price_snapshot'][product_name],
                        stock=quantity
                    )
                except Exception as e:
                    # Log error but continue with other products
                    print(f"Error recreating product {product_name}: {str(e)}")

        return True

    # Handle status transition for non-cancellation cases
    valid_transitions = {
        'pending': ['processing'],
        'processing': ['completed'],
        'completed': ['shipped'],
        'shipped': ['delivered']
    }

    # Check if transition is valid and order is not in terminal state
    if (current_status in valid_transitions and
        new_status in valid_transitions[current_status] and
        current_status not in ['cancelled', 'delivered']):

        order['status'] = new_status
        return True

    return False
===============================


===== generate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Generated code for EShopSystem.get_system_state: 
@classmethod
def get_system_state(cls, entity_type):
    """
    Retrieves the current state of users, products, stock, carts, or orders.

    Args:
        entity_type (str): The type of entity to retrieve (e.g., 'users', 'products', 'stock', 'carts', 'orders').

    Returns:
        dict: The current state of the requested entity type, or an empty dictionary if the entity type is invalid.
              For 'stock', returns current available stock excluding reserved quantities.
    """
    if entity_type == 'users':
        return cls.users.copy()
    elif entity_type == 'products':
        return cls.products.copy()
    elif entity_type == 'stock':
        return cls.available_stock.copy()
    elif entity_type == 'carts':
        carts = {}
        for user_id, user_data in cls.users.items():
            carts[user_id] = user_data.get('cart', {})
        return carts
    elif entity_type == 'orders':
        return cls.orders.copy()
    else:
        return {}

===== regenerate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Regenerated code for EShopSystem.checkout: 
@classmethod
def checkout(cls, user_id):
    # Step 1: Validate user exists and cart is not empty
    if user_id not in cls.users or not cls.users[user_id]['cart']:
        return False, None

    # Step 2: Validate all reserved products exist
    for product_name in cls.users[user_id]['cart']:
        if product_name not in cls.products:
            return False, None

    # Step 3: Calculate total price
    total_price = 0.0
    for product_name, quantity in cls.users[user_id]['cart'].items():
        total_price += cls.products[product_name]['price'] * quantity

    # Step 4: Generate order ID
    order_id = f"SK{cls.next_order_id:05d}"

    # Store original state for rollback
    original_cart = cls.users[user_id]['cart'].copy()
    original_reservations = cls.reservations.get(user_id, {}).copy()

    # Step 5: Begin atomic transaction
    try:
        # Create order
        cls.orders[order_id] = {
            'status': 'pending',
            'products': cls.users[user_id]['cart'].copy(),
            'price_snapshot': {p: cls.products[p]['price'] for p in cls.users[user_id]['cart']},
            'total_price': total_price
        }

        # Clear cart and reservations
        cls.users[user_id]['cart'].clear()
        if user_id in cls.reservations:
            for product_name in cls.reservations[user_id]:
                cls.available_stock[product_name] += cls.reservations[user_id][product_name]
            del cls.reservations[user_id]

        # Increment order ID for next use
        cls.next_order_id += 1

        return True, order_id

    except Exception:
        # Rollback all changes
        if order_id in cls.orders:
            del cls.orders[order_id]

        # Restore original cart and reservations
        cls.users[user_id]['cart'] = original_cart
        if original_reservations:
            cls.reservations[user_id] = original_reservations
            for product_name, quantity in original_reservations.items():
                if product_name in cls.available_stock:
                    cls.available_stock[product_name] -= quantity

        cls.next_order_id -= 1  # Rollback order ID generation
        return False, None
===============================


===== regenerate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Regenerated code for EShopSystem.update_order_status: 
def update_order_status(self, order_id, new_status):
    # Validate order exists
    if order_id not in self.orders:
        return False

    order = self.orders[order_id]
    current_status = order['status']

    # Handle cancellation case
    if new_status == 'cancelled':
        if current_status in ['cancelled', 'delivered']:
            return False

        # Update order status
        order['status'] = 'cancelled'

        # Process each product in the order
        for product_name, quantity in order['products'].items():
            if product_name in self.products:
                # Initialize available_stock if not exists
                if product_name not in self.available_stock:
                    self.available_stock[product_name] = 0
                # Increase available stock for existing product
                self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity
            else:
                # Recreate product with order's price and set stock to ordered quantity
                try:
                    self.add_or_update_product(
                        name=product_name,
                        price=order['price_snapshot'][product_name],
                        stock=quantity
                    )
                except Exception as e:
                    # Log error but continue with other products
                    print(f"Error recreating product {product_name}: {str(e)}")

        # Confirm cancellation
        return True

    # Handle status transition for non-cancellation cases
    valid_transitions = {
        'pending': ['processing'],
        'processing': ['completed'],
        'completed': ['shipped'],
        'shipped': ['delivered']
    }

    # Check if transition is valid and order is not in terminal state
    if (current_status in valid_transitions and
        new_status in valid_transitions[current_status] and
        current_status not in ['cancelled', 'delivered']):

        order['status'] = new_status
        return True

    return False

Regenerated code for EShopSystem.delete_product: 
def delete_product(self, name):
    """
    Removes a product from the current available stock system while preserving existing reservations and orders.

    Args:
        name (str): The name of the product to be deleted.

    Returns:
        bool: True if the product was deleted successfully, False otherwise.
    """
    if name not in self.products:
        return False

    # Remove from available stock
    if name in self.available_stock:
        del self.available_stock[name]

    # Remove from total stock
    del self.products[name]

    # Remove from reservations if present
    for user_id in self.reservations:
        if name in self.reservations[user_id]:
            del self.reservations[user_id][name]

    return True
===============================


===== regenerate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Regenerated code for EShopSystem.remove_from_cart: 
def remove_from_cart(self, user_id, product_name, quantity):
    # Validate inputs
    if quantity <= 0:
        return False

    # Check if user exists
    if user_id not in self.users:
        return False

    # Check if product is in cart and quantity is valid
    user_cart = self.users[user_id].get('cart', {})
    if product_name not in user_cart or user_cart[product_name] < quantity:
        return False

    # Check if product exists in the system
    if product_name not in self.products:
        return False

    # Update cart
    if user_cart[product_name] == quantity:
        del user_cart[product_name]
    else:
        user_cart[product_name] -= quantity

    # Update reservations
    if user_id in self.reservations and product_name in self.reservations.get(user_id, {}):
        if self.reservations[user_id][product_name] == quantity:
            del self.reservations[user_id][product_name]
        else:
            self.reservations[user_id][product_name] -= quantity

    # Return stock to available_stock
    if product_name in self.available_stock:
        self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity

    return True

===== regenerate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Regenerated code for EShopSystem.checkout: 
@classmethod
def checkout(cls, user_id):
    # Step 1: Validate user exists and cart is not empty
    if user_id not in cls.users or not cls.users[user_id]['cart']:
        return False, None

    # Step 2: Validate all reserved products exist
    for product_name in cls.users[user_id]['cart']:
        if product_name not in cls.products:
            return False, None

    # Step 3: Calculate total price
    total_price = 0.0
    for product_name, quantity in cls.users[user_id]['cart'].items():
        total_price += cls.products[product_name]['price'] * quantity

    # Step 4: Generate order ID
    order_id = f"SK{cls.next_order_id:05d}"

    # Store original state for rollback
    original_cart = cls.users[user_id]['cart'].copy()
    original_reservations = cls.reservations.get(user_id, {}).copy()
    original_available_stock = {}
    for product_name in original_reservations:
        original_available_stock[product_name] = cls.available_stock.get(product_name, 0)

    # Step 5: Begin atomic transaction
    try:
        # Create order
        cls.orders[order_id] = {
            'status': 'pending',
            'products': cls.users[user_id]['cart'].copy(),
            'price_snapshot': {p: cls.products[p]['price'] for p in cls.users[user_id]['cart']},
            'total_price': total_price
        }

        # Clear cart and reservations
        cls.users[user_id]['cart'].clear()
        if user_id in cls.reservations:
            for product_name in cls.reservations[user_id]:
                cls.available_stock[product_name] += cls.reservations[user_id][product_name]
            del cls.reservations[user_id]

        # Increment order ID for next use
        cls.next_order_id += 1

        return True, order_id

    except Exception:
        # Rollback all changes
        if order_id in cls.orders:
            del cls.orders[order_id]

        # Restore original cart and reservations
        cls.users[user_id]['cart'] = original_cart
        if original_reservations:
            cls.reservations[user_id] = original_reservations
            for product_name, quantity in original_reservations.items():
                if product_name in cls.available_stock:
                    cls.available_stock[product_name] = original_available_stock.get(product_name, 0)

        cls.next_order_id -= 1  # Rollback order ID generation
        return False, None
===============================


===== regenerate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Regenerated code for EShopSystem.update_order_status: 
def update_order_status(self, order_id, new_status):
    # Validate order exists
    if order_id not in self.orders:
        return False

    order = self.orders[order_id]
    current_status = order['status']

    # Handle cancellation case
    if new_status == 'cancelled':
        if current_status in ['cancelled', 'delivered']:
            return False

        # Update order status
        order['status'] = 'cancelled'

        # Process each product in the order
        for product_name, quantity in order['products'].items():
            if product_name in self.products:
                # Ensure available_stock exists for the product
                if product_name not in self.available_stock:
                    self.available_stock[product_name] = 0
                # Increase available stock for existing product
                self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity
            else:
                # Recreate product with order's price and set stock to ordered quantity
                try:
                    self.add_or_update_product(
                        name=product_name,
                        price=order['price_snapshot'][product_name],
                        stock=quantity
                    )
                except Exception as e:
                    # Log error but continue with other products
                    print(f"Error recreating product {product_name}: {str(e)}")
                    # Mandatory recreation - if failed, return False to indicate failure
                    return False

        # Confirm cancellation
        return True

    # Handle status transition for non-cancellation cases
    valid_transitions = {
        'pending': ['processing'],
        'processing': ['completed'],
        'completed': ['shipped'],
        'shipped': ['delivered']
    }

    # Check if transition is valid and order is not in terminal state
    if (current_status in valid_transitions and
        new_status in valid_transitions[current_status] and
        current_status not in ['cancelled', 'delivered']):

        order['status'] = new_status
        return True

    return False

    if product_name not in self.products:

@classmethod
def checkout(cls, user_id):
    # Step 1: Validate user exists and cart is not empty
    if user_id not in cls.users or not cls.users[user_id]['cart']:
        return False, None

    # Step 2: Validate all reserved products exist
    for product_name in cls.users[user_id]['cart']:
        if product_name not in cls.products:
            return False, None

    # Step 3: Calculate total price
    total_price = 0.0
    for product_name, quantity in cls.users[user_id]['cart'].items():
        total_price += cls.products[product_name]['price'] * quantity

    # Step 4: Generate order ID
    order_id = f"SK{cls.next_order_id:05d}"

    # Store original state for rollback
    original_cart = cls.users[user_id]['cart'].copy()
    original_reservations = cls.reservations.get(user_id, {}).copy()
    original_available_stock = {}
    for product_name in original_reservations:
        original_available_stock[product_name] = cls.available_stock.get(product_name, 0)

    # Step 5: Begin atomic transaction
    try:
        # Create order
        cls.orders[order_id] = {
            'status': 'pending',
            'products': cls.users[user_id]['cart'].copy(),
            'price_snapshot': {p: cls.products[p]['price'] for p in cls.users[user_id]['cart']},
            'total_price': total_price
        }

        # Clear cart and reservations
        cls.users[user_id]['cart'].clear()
        if user_id in cls.reservations:
            for product_name in cls.reservations[user_id]:
                cls.available_stock[product_name] += cls.reservations[user_id][product_name]
            del cls.reservations[user_id]

        # Increment order ID for next use
        cls.next_order_id += 1

        return True, order_id

    except Exception:
        # Rollback all changes
        if order_id in cls.orders:
            del cls.orders[order_id]

        # Restore original cart and reservations
        cls.users[user_id]['cart'] = original_cart
        if original_reservations:
            cls.reservations[user_id] = original_reservations
            for product_name, quantity in original_reservations.items():
                if product_name in cls.available_stock:
                    cls.available_stock[product_name] = original_available_stock.get(product_name, 0)

        # Only decrement next_order_id if it was incremented during the transaction
        if cls.next_order_id > 1:  # Assuming initial next_order_id is 1
            cls.next_order_id -= 1

        return False, None

Regenerated code for EShopSystem.delete_user: 
def delete_user(self, user_id):
    if user_id not in self.users:
        return False

    # Cancel all reservations for the user
    if user_id in self.reservations:
        for product_name, quantity in self.reservations[user_id].items():
            if product_name in self.available_stock:
                self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity
        del self.reservations[user_id]

    # Remove user's cart
    if 'cart' in self.users[user_id]:
        del self.users[user_id]['cart']

    # Delete the user (orders remain unchanged)
    del self.users[user_id]

    return True

Regenerated code for EShopSystem.add_or_update_product: 
def add_or_update_product(self, name, price, stock):
    # Validate inputs
    if not name or price < 0 or stock < 0:
        return False

    if name in self.products:
        # Update existing product
        self.products[name]['price'] = price
        self.products[name]['total_stock'] += stock
        self.available_stock[name] += stock
    else:
        # Add new product
        self.products[name] = {
            'price': price,
            'total_stock': stock
        }
        self.available_stock[name] = stock

    return True

def checkout(self, user_id):
    # Step 1: Validate user exists and cart is not empty
    if user_id not in self.users or not self.users[user_id]['cart']:
        return False, None

    # Step 2: Validate all reserved products exist
    for product_name in self.users[user_id]['cart']:
        if product_name not in self.products:
            return False, None

    # Step 3: Calculate total price
    total_price = 0.0
    for product_name, quantity in self.users[user_id]['cart'].items():
        total_price += self.products[product_name]['price'] * quantity

    # Step 4: Generate order ID
    order_id = f"SK{self.next_order_id:05d}"

    # Store original state for rollback
    original_cart = self.users[user_id]['cart'].copy()
    original_reservations = self.reservations.get(user_id, {}).copy()
    original_available_stock = {}
    for product_name in original_reservations:
        original_available_stock[product_name] = self.available_stock.get(product_name, 0)

    # Step 5: Begin atomic transaction
    try:
        # Create order
        self.orders[order_id] = {
            'status': 'pending',
            'products': self.users[user_id]['cart'].copy(),
            'price_snapshot': {p: self.products[p]['price'] for p in self.users[user_id]['cart']},
            'total_price': total_price
        }

        # Clear cart and reservations
        self.users[user_id]['cart'].clear()
        if user_id in self.reservations:
            for product_name in self.reservations[user_id]:
                self.available_stock[product_name] += self.reservations[user_id][product_name]
            del self.reservations[user_id]

        # Increment order ID for next use
        self.next_order_id += 1

        return True, order_id

    except Exception:
        # Rollback all changes
        if order_id in self.orders:
            del self.orders[order_id]

        # Restore original cart and reservations
        self.users[user_id]['cart'] = original_cart
        if original_reservations:
            self.reservations[user_id] = original_reservations
            for product_name, quantity in original_reservations.items():
                if product_name in self.available_stock:
                    self.available_stock[product_name] = original_available_stock.get(product_name, 0)

        # Only decrement next_order_id if it was incremented during the transaction
        if self.next_order_id > 1:  # Assuming initial next_order_id is 1
            self.next_order_id -= 1

        return False, None

===== regenerate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Regenerated code for EShopSystem.get_system_state: 
def get_system_state(self, entity_type):
    """
    Retrieves the current state of users, products, stock, carts, or orders.

    Args:
        entity_type (str): The type of entity to retrieve (e.g., 'users', 'products', 'stock', 'carts', 'orders').

    Returns:
        dict: The current state of the requested entity type, or an empty dictionary if the entity type is invalid.
              For 'stock', returns current available stock excluding reserved quantities.
    """
    if entity_type == 'users':
        return self.users.copy()
    elif entity_type == 'products':
        return self.products.copy()
    elif entity_type == 'stock':
        return self.available_stock.copy()
    elif entity_type == 'carts':
        carts = {}
        for user_id, user_data in self.users.items():
            carts[user_id] = user_data.get('cart', {})
        return carts
    elif entity_type == 'orders':
        return self.orders.copy()
    else:
        return {}

Regenerated code for EShopSystem.add_to_cart: 
def add_to_cart(self, user_id, product_name, quantity):
    # Validate user exists
    if user_id not in self.users:
        return False

    # Validate product exists and quantity is valid
    if product_name not in self.products or quantity <= 0:
        return False

    # Check available stock
    if product_name not in self.available_stock or self.available_stock[product_name] < quantity:
        return False

    # Update cart
    if product_name in self.users[user_id]['cart']:
        self.users[user_id]['cart'][product_name] += quantity
    else:
        self.users[user_id]['cart'][product_name] = quantity

    # Update reservations
    if product_name in self.reservations[user_id]:
        self.reservations[user_id][product_name] += quantity
    else:
        self.reservations[user_id][product_name] = quantity

    # Deduct from available stock
    self.available_stock[product_name] -= quantity

    return True

===== regenerate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Regenerated code for EShopSystem.checkout: 
def checkout(self, user_id):
    # Step 1: Validate user exists and cart is not empty
    if user_id not in self.users or not self.users[user_id]['cart']:
        return False, None

    # Step 2: Validate all reserved products exist
    for product_name in self.users[user_id]['cart']:
        if product_name not in self.products:
            return False, None

    # Step 3: Calculate total price
    total_price = 0.0
    for product_name, quantity in self.users[user_id]['cart'].items():
        total_price += self.products[product_name]['price'] * quantity

    # Store original state for rollback
    original_cart = self.users[user_id]['cart'].copy()
    original_reservations = self.reservations.get(user_id, {}).copy()
    original_available_stock = {}
    for product_name in original_reservations:
        original_available_stock[product_name] = self.available_stock.get(product_name, 0)

    # Step 4: Generate order ID
    order_id = f"SK{self.next_order_id:05d}"

    # Step 5: Begin atomic transaction
    try:
        # Create order
        self.orders[order_id] = {
            'status': 'pending',
            'products': self.users[user_id]['cart'].copy(),
            'price_snapshot': {p: self.products[p]['price'] for p in self.users[user_id]['cart']},
            'total_price': total_price
        }

        # Clear cart and reservations
        self.users[user_id]['cart'].clear()
        if user_id in self.reservations:
            for product_name in self.reservations[user_id]:
                self.available_stock[product_name] += self.reservations[user_id][product_name]
            del self.reservations[user_id]

        # Increment order ID for next use
        self.next_order_id += 1

        return True, order_id

    except Exception:
        # Rollback all changes
        if order_id in self.orders:
            del self.orders[order_id]

        # Restore original cart and reservations
        self.users[user_id]['cart'] = original_cart
        if original_reservations:
            self.reservations[user_id] = original_reservations
            for product_name, quantity in original_reservations.items():
                if product_name in self.available_stock:
                    self.available_stock[product_name] = original_available_stock.get(product_name, 0)

        # Rollback next_order_id
        if self.next_order_id > 1:  # Assuming initial next_order_id is 1
            self.next_order_id -= 1

        return False, None
===============================


===== regenerate_single_method OUTPUT =====
Regenerated code for EShopSystem.update_order_status: 
def update_order_status(self, order_id, new_status):
    # Validate order exists
    if order_id not in self.orders:
        return False

    order = self.orders[order_id]
    current_status = order['status']

    # Handle cancellation case
    if new_status == 'cancelled':
        if current_status in ['cancelled', 'delivered']:
            return False

        # Update order status
        order['status'] = 'cancelled'

        # Process each product in the order
        for product_name, quantity in order['products'].items():
            if product_name in self.products:
                # Ensure available_stock exists for the product
                if product_name not in self.available_stock:
                    self.available_stock[product_name] = 0
                # Increase available stock for existing product
                self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity
            else:
                # Recreate product with order's price and set stock to ordered quantity
                try:
                    # Validate product recreation parameters
                    if not product_name or order['price_snapshot'][product_name] < 0 or quantity < 0:
                        raise ValueError("Invalid product recreation parameters")
                    self.add_or_update_product(
                        name=product_name,
                        price=order['price_snapshot'][product_name],
                        stock=quantity
                    )
                except Exception as e:
                    # Log error but continue with other products
                    print(f"Error recreating product {product_name}: {str(e)}")
                    # Continue with other products even if recreation fails

        # Confirm cancellation
        return True

    # Handle status transition for non-cancellation cases
    valid_transitions = {
        'pending': ['processing'],
        'processing': ['completed'],
        'completed': ['shipped'],
        'shipped': ['delivered']
    }

    # Check if transition is valid and order is not in terminal state
    if (current_status in valid_transitions and
        new_status in valid_transitions[current_status] and
        current_status not in ['cancelled', 'delivered']):

        order['status'] = new_status
        return True

    return False

Regenerated code for EShopSystem.get_system_state: 
def get_system_state(self, entity_type):
    """
    Retrieves the current state of users, products, stock, carts, or orders.

    Args:
        entity_type (str): The type of entity to retrieve (e.g., 'users', 'products', 'stock', 'carts', 'orders').

    Returns:
        dict: The current state of the requested entity type, or an empty dictionary if the entity type is invalid.
              For 'stock', returns current available stock excluding reserved quantities.
    """
    if entity_type == 'users':
        return {user_id: user_data.copy() for user_id, user_data in self.users.items()}
    elif entity_type == 'products':
        return self.products.copy()
    elif entity_type == 'stock':
        return self.available_stock.copy()
    elif entity_type == 'carts':
        carts = {}
        for user_id, user_data in self.users.items():
            carts[user_id] = user_data.get('cart', {}).copy()
        return carts
    elif entity_type == 'orders':
        return self.orders.copy()
    else:
        return {}

def delete_user(self, user_id):
    if user_id not in self.users:
        return False

    # Cancel all reservations for the user
    if user_id in self.reservations:
        for product_name, quantity in self.reservations[user_id].items():
            if product_name in self.available_stock:
                self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity
        del self.reservations[user_id]

    # Remove user's cart and preserve other user details
    if 'cart' in self.users[user_id]:
        del self.users[user_id]['cart']

    # Delete the user (orders remain unchanged)
    del self.users[user_id]

    return True

def delete_product(self, name):
    """
    Removes a product from the current available stock system while preserving existing reservations and orders.

    Args:
        name (str): The name of the product to be deleted.

    Returns:
        bool: True if the product was deleted successfully, False otherwise.
    """
    if name not in self.products:
        return False

    # Remove from available stock
    if name in self.available_stock:
        del self.available_stock[name]

    # Remove from total stock
    del self.products[name]

    # Preserve reservations and orders by not modifying them
    return True

Regenerated code for EShopSystem.remove_from_cart: 
def remove_from_cart(self, user_id, product_name, quantity):
    # Validate inputs
    if quantity <= 0:
        return False

    # Check if user exists
    if user_id not in self.users:
        return False

    # Check if product exists in the system
    if product_name not in self.products:
        return False

    # Check if product is in cart and quantity is valid
    user_cart = self.users[user_id].get('cart', {})
    if product_name not in user_cart or user_cart[product_name] < quantity:
        return False

    # Update cart
    if user_cart[product_name] == quantity:
        del user_cart[product_name]
    else:
        user_cart[product_name] -= quantity

    # Update reservations
    if user_id in self.reservations:
        if product_name in self.reservations[user_id]:
            if self.reservations[user_id][product_name] == quantity:
                del self.reservations[user_id][product_name]
            else:
                self.reservations[user_id][product_name] -= quantity

    # Return stock to available_stock only if product exists in available_stock
    if product_name in self.available_stock:
        self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity

    return True

def checkout(self, user_id):
    # Step 1: Validate user exists and cart is not empty
    if user_id not in self.users or not self.users[user_id]['cart']:
        return False, None

    # Step 2: Validate all reserved products exist
    for product_name in self.users[user_id]['cart']:
        if product_name not in self.products:
            return False, None

    # Step 3: Calculate total price
    total_price = 0.0
    for product_name, quantity in self.users[user_id]['cart'].items():
        total_price += self.products[product_name]['price'] * quantity

    # Store original state for rollback
    original_cart = self.users[user_id]['cart'].copy()
    original_reservations = self.reservations.get(user_id, {}).copy()
    original_available_stock = {}
    for product_name in original_reservations:
        original_available_stock[product_name] = self.available_stock.get(product_name, 0)

    # Step 4: Generate order ID
    order_id = f"SK{self.next_order_id:05d}"

    # Store original next_order_id for rollback
    original_next_order_id = self.next_order_id

    # Step 5: Begin atomic transaction
    try:
        # Create order
        self.orders[order_id] = {
            'status': 'pending',
            'products': self.users[user_id]['cart'].copy(),
            'price_snapshot': {p: self.products[p]['price'] for p in self.users[user_id]['cart']},
            'total_price': total_price
        }

        # Clear cart and reservations
        self.users[user_id]['cart'].clear()
        if user_id in self.reservations:
            for product_name in self.reservations[user_id]:
                self.available_stock[product_name] += self.reservations[user_id][product_name]
            del self.reservations[user_id]

        # Increment order ID for next use
        self.next_order_id += 1

        return True, order_id

    except Exception:

        if order_id in self.orders:
            del self.orders[order_id]

        # Restore original cart and reservations
        self.users[user_id]['cart'] = original_cart

        # Rollback next_order_id

def update_order_status(self, order_id, new_status):
    # Validate order exists
    if order_id not in self.orders:
        return False

    order = self.orders[order_id]
    current_status = order['status']

    # Handle cancellation case
    if new_status == 'cancelled':
        if current_status in ['cancelled', 'delivered']:
            return False

        # Update order status
        order['status'] = 'cancelled'

        # Process each product in the order
        for product_name, quantity in order['products'].items():
            if product_name in self.products:
                # Ensure available_stock exists for the product
                if product_name not in self.available_stock:
                    self.available_stock[product_name] = 0
                # Increase available stock for existing product
                self.available_stock[product_name] += quantity
            else:
                # Recreate product with order's price and set stock to ordered quantity
                try:
                    # Validate product recreation parameters
                    if not product_name or order['price_snapshot'][product_name] < 0 or quantity < 0:
                        raise ValueError("Invalid product recreation parameters")
                    # Ensure product recreation is mandatory
                    if not self.add_or_update_product(
                        name=product_name,
                        price=order['price_snapshot'][product_name],
                        stock=quantity
                    ):
                        raise ValueError("Failed to recreate product")
                except Exception as e:
                    # Log error but continue with other products
                    print(f"Error recreating product {product_name}: {str(e)}")
                    # Continue with other products even if recreation fails

        # Confirm cancellation
        return True

    # Handle status transition for non-cancellation cases
    valid_transitions = {
        'pending': ['processing'],
        'processing': ['completed'],
        'completed': ['shipped'],
        'shipped': ['delivered']
    }

    # Check if transition is valid and order is not in terminal state
    if (current_status in valid_transitions and
        new_status in valid_transitions[current_status] and
        current_status not in ['cancelled', 'delivered']):

        order['status'] = new_status
        return True
